市場焦點
美國
• 9月CPI年增3.7%、PPI年增6.3%，雙雙高於前期 ，能源價格的急升是通膨回升的主要推力 。
雖然核心CPI年增率僅升至2.6%，但住房與服務價格仍維持黏著，使通膨降溫過程出現反覆；
同時PPI增幅明顯高於 CPI，反映企業成本壓力偏高 ，未來不排除進一步向終端價格傳導 。整
體而言，CPI與PPI同步回升反映通膨降溫進程仍存在不確定性 ，進而令市場重估通膨回落速
度與對Fed貨幣政策路徑的審慎預期。
AI
• AI雲端業者 CoreWeave的5年期CDS利差一度突破 1,140個基點 ，旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近
15%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延至股票市場 。全球最大半導體設備商ASML表示，客戶已開始洽
談2029至2030年的訂單；晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期 。與此同時，台灣出口增

速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季
相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。
中國
• 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力
仍在，彭博表示以GDP平減指數衡量 ，經歷三年下滑後 ，整體經濟預計將在 2026年走出通縮 ，
投資上短期可聚焦，受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。

美股獲利引擎轉向半導體/核心AI與能源
• 雖然標普500指數在2026年第三季展現出獲利韌性 ，但隨著油價上漲侵蝕利潤率 、消費者受通膨壓抑 ，以及分析師下調未
來預測動能，市場未來的容錯空間正在縮小。
• AI與半導體仍為獲利領頭羊：科技與半導體持續為標普500指數獲利成長的核心動力，其中約20家企業組成的核心AI一籃子
股票(權重約34.6%)對指數獲利的貢獻度已超過科技七雄(Mag 7)，且此優勢預計將至少延續至2028年底。
• 油價推升能源產業，接棒成為強棒：受到近期國際油價上揚影響，能源產業獲利預測顯著上修，預計獲利成長勁道將延續至
2027年初。在接下來的三個季度中，能源板塊的收益成長表現預計將超越科技七雄。
• 消費與運輸業面臨成本壓頂：相較於科技與能源的強勁，必需消費品與交通運輸業正承受顯著壓力。例如，達美航空受高燃

料成本拖累，而Nike與百事可樂則反映出消費者因持續通膨而縮減支出的困境。
7月以來EPS和營收成長預期修正
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
剔除能源、科技七雄和AI一籃子股票後的指數收益成長

顧
利潤率高檔盤整、關稅退款帶來短補，但動能已見放緩
• 利潤率高檔盤整，淨利潤率提升受限：標普500成分股第二季營運利潤率(18.7%)與淨利潤率(15.6%)均創下新高。雖然第三
季營運利潤率預估可進一步提升至19.4%，但淨利潤率預計微幅上升至15.8%，顯示整體企業淨利潤的擴張動能正漸趨平緩 。
• 700億美元關稅退款提供短線補藥： 受惠於美國政府依據國際緊急經濟權力法 (IEEPA)的追償退稅，估計仍有至少700億美元
的關稅款將退還給標普 500成分股(主要集中於工業、非必需消費及醫療保健等 )，這預計能為2026年標普500指數每股收益
(EPS)成長帶來約235個基點、利潤率提升約34個基點的短期提振。
• 上修動能開始轉弱：雖然整體財報指引動量仍維持正值，但過去六週內，分析師對未來12個月EPS與收入的修正動能出現頻

繁下修，這意味著市場先前的極度樂觀情緒正在消退，未來財報季度要優於預期的門檻正在提高。
• 小結與投資建議： 標普500指數短期內靠著半導體 /AI與能源雙引擎拉抬 ，加上關稅退款的一次性利多注入 ，下半年整體獲
利依然具備支撐。然而，高油價帶來的營運成本上升 ，以及終端消費力道的疲軟 ，正逐漸侵蝕非科技/非能源企業的利潤 ，
而大盤廣度不足(僅少數板塊支撐獲利)，代表未來投資風險正逐漸集中 ，但AI算力需求仍屬硬性需求，但波動加劇下，投資
建議以定期定額方式投資美股核心AI與半導體龍頭股票。
利潤率預測
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
各產業第二季度收到的關稅退款
未來12月EPS預期修正動能與營收

顧
美國9月CPI、PPI 同步升溫，能源衝擊擴大通膨與利率風險
• 能源價格急升，消費通膨再升溫：美國9月CPI月增0.6%、
年增3.7%，高於前月 3.4%，其中汽油月增 9.1%、能源
價格月增 5.2%，汽油單項即貢獻整體 CPI當月漲幅逾半；
另一方面，核心CPI月增0.3%、年增2.6%、住房指數月
增0.4%，顯示能源雖是本次通膨回升主因 ，但住房及部
分服務價格仍具黏著性，令通膨降溫進程面臨反覆。
• 上游價格大幅攀升 ，成本傳導風險升高： 9月PPI最終需
求月增1.3%、年增6.3%，創2022年3月以來最大增幅，
其中最終需求商品月增 2.6%，能源價格大漲 10.8%，汽
油價格更跳升 20.3%；核心PPI月增0.5%、年增5.0%，
顯示價格壓力不僅來自能源 ，也涉及部分商品與服務環

節；後續是否進一步推升至 CPI，仍須觀察企業成本轉
嫁程度及需求變化。
• 市場看法： 通膨回升恐提高 Fed再度升息機率 ，並延長
高利率維持時間 ，甚至不排除進一步緊縮的可能 ，短期
有利支撐美債殖利率及美元表現 ，不過股、債市後續走
勢仍將受到貨幣政策預期與風險情緒變化影響 。資產配
置建議可關注具成本轉嫁能力 、現金流穩健的能源類股；
債券則宜以短中天期 、高信用評等標的為主 ，以降低利
率敏感度。黃金可作為分散地緣政治及通膨風險的輔助
配置，但不宜將任何單一資產視為必然受惠標的。
CPI 與核心 CPI 走勢變動

顧
供給面推動中國9月CPI升0.9%
• 供給面推動中國9月通膨復甦：中國9月通膨數據顯示，CPI年增率由8月的0.8%升至0.9%，連續兩個月擴大，但低於市場預期
的1.0%，物價上漲主要受到能源及旅遊價格帶動，而食品價格跌幅進一步擴大，使整體通膨壓力仍相對溫和，扣除食品與能源
後，核心CPI維持在1.0%，反映內需需求尚未明顯升溫 。另一方面，PPI年增率由3.8%大幅升至5.0%，優於市場預期，且月增
率達1.2%，創4月以來最大漲幅，顯示上游原物料及工業品價格回升，須關注未來是否逐步傳導至消費端價格。
• 展望後市： 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求 ，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力仍 在，彭博表示 以
GDP平減指數衡量，經歷三年下滑後，整體經濟預計將在2026年走出通縮，值得注意的是，這種供給面驅動的通膨復甦，將降

低官方推出需求刺激措施的緊迫感 ，而要反轉通縮循環正需要官方舉措，投資上短期可聚焦受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的
半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.14
通膨集中於能源和AI相關行業

顧
過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數
的兩倍
• 過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數的兩倍：主因為獲利帶動成長，整體而言，新興市場企
業獲利表現強勁 ，而且仍持續加速成長 ，高盛預估2026年新興市場企業盈餘成長率將達 55%，超過標普500企業盈餘成長速
度的兩倍，且新興市場股票估值較美國股市存在超過40%的折價空間，評價相對具吸引力，今年以來流入新興市場股市的資金
已接近500億美元，創下過去20年來最強勁紀錄之一。更重要的是新興市場國家目前貢獻了全球近 80%的AI硬體出口。隨著AI
產業持續發展，受惠範圍有望從少數龍頭企業擴大至更多國家及公司，特別是那些能夠協助解決供應鏈瓶頸的企業。
• 結論：後市來看在AI產業趨勢、相對低估的評價水準，以及強勁企業獲利成長三大因素支撐下，新興市場股票未來一年仍具備

進一步上漲的潛力，有機會延續目前的強勢表現。

AI投資支撐美國成長，融資需求升高使長端利率與信用風險並存
• AI基礎建設持續擴張 ，成為企業投資重要動能 ：大型雲端業者
持續投入資料中心 、半導體、網路設備與電力基礎設施 ，帶動
相關產業資本支出與供應鏈需求 。摩根大通資產管理指出 ，納
入資料中心開發商及半導體投資後，AI 基礎建設至 2030 年的
累計投資需求可能達約 5兆美元 ，顯示AI投資循環仍具規模 ，
但實際經濟貢獻與企業獲利 ，仍取決於投資效率及商業化成果 。
• 外部融資需求增加 ，債券供給與投資回報成關鍵： 隨AI投資規
模擴大，大型科技企業逐步增加公司債與外部融資 ，在企業債
與公債供給同步增加下 ，長天期利率面臨上行壓力 。若未來AI
獲利成長未能跟上資本支出擴張速度 ，可能壓縮自由現金流與

資本報酬率，進一步擴大信用利差 ，並提高高估值科技股的評
價修正風險
• 市場觀察：AI基礎建設仍是未來數年推動全球科技投資與企業
數位轉型的成長主軸，相關需求具備中長期發展空間，而2026
年市場已逐漸轉向檢視投資回報率 、現金流創造能力及融資結
構是否具備可持續性 ，因此單純依賴資本支出擴張已不足以支
撐所有AI概念股評價 。建議配置可兼顧具獲利與現金流支撐的
AI供應鏈，以及高信用評等 、中短天期債券；對長天期債券則
須評估供給與利率風險 ，並審慎檢視高負債 AI企業的償債能力
及投資回報。
資料來源：Goldman Sachs，永豐金證券， 2026.10.12

AI熱潮、高債務與網路威脅，IMF示警三大金融穩定風險升高
資料來源：Bloomberg Finance L.P， Dealogic，Zero day Clock and IMF staff calculations.， 2026.10.13
AI相關風險持續升溫 主權債券殖利率攀升 網路資安威脅升高
大型雲端科技企業債券發行規模
（占投資等級公司債市場比重) 10年期公債殖利率平均變動幅度 零時差漏洞占比（%）
• AI估值高度集中，市場重定價風險升溫：IMF於10月《全球金融穩定報告》指出，AI相關投資已成為全球成長的重要支撐，
但資金與漲勢高度集中於少數企業及市場，一旦獲利成長不如預期或估值遭重新檢視，可能引發科技股修正並透過風險偏好
下降擴散至整體金融市場。
• 通膨與財政疑慮恐推升債券市場波動： IMF認為，若能源價格推升通膨壓力或市場對財政永續性產生疑慮 ，主權債殖利率可

能進一步上升，進而提高全球融資成本與資產重估風險；在高利率與高債務環境下，金融條件快速緊縮的情況將明顯提高。
• 網路安全風險成為潛在威脅： 隨金融體系數位化程度提高 ，IMF將大型網路攻擊列為重要風險來源之一 。若攻擊事件波及支
付系統、金融基礎設施或雲端服務平台，不僅可能影響市場運作效率，也可能透過信心衝擊與流動性壓力擴大金融市場波動。
• 投資建議：市場成長動能仍受AI支撐，但在金融穩定風險上升情境下，建議採取「AI受惠股＋高品質債券＋防禦型資產」的
槓鈴策略；核心配置可聚焦全球AI基礎建設供應鏈及投資等級債，藉由企業長期獲利成長與債券避險功能，同時因應估值修
正及金融條件收緊風險。

AI隱憂浮現但動能仍在
• CoreWeave信用風險亮紅燈：市場憂心雲端業者在算力租賃業務產生足夠回報前，恐難以負擔龐大債務，導致信用風險升
溫。上週，AI雲端業者CoreWeave的5年期信用違約交換（CDS）利差一度突破1,140個基點，升至通常反映嚴重信用壓力
的水準；旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近15%，其中2032年到期的無擔保債券於上週三跌破面值的80%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延
至股票市場。
• AI半導體產業的基本面仍具韌性：全球最大半導體設備商艾司摩爾（ASML）表示，客戶已開始洽談2029至2030年的訂單；
晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期。與此同時，台灣出口增速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯
示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。

美銀美林調查指出，全球經理人對景氣與股市仍維持偏多立場
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10
• 經理人樂觀情緒維持高檔： 美銀美林10月全球基金經理人調查顯示 ，市場風險偏好仍高 ，平均現金水位維持在 3.9%的低檔，
科技股配置則升至2020年8月以來最高，反映經理人對AI產業及企業獲利前景仍具信心 。然而，整體經濟成長預期已轉趨保
守，淨4%的受訪者預期未來 12個月經濟成長放緩 。極端風險方面，債券殖利率無序攀升仍居首位 ，占比31%；AI泡沫及通
膨再起分別為23%及20%。此外，39%的經理人認為，大型雲端業者資本支出是系統性信用事件的主要潛在來源 ，顯示市場
焦點已從AI需求成長延伸至龐大資本支出的獲利回收能力。

• 美國公債殖利率走高 ，債券的收益吸引力逐漸回升： 調查顯示，37%的受訪者表示，當美國10年期公債殖利率升至 6%時，
才會考慮由股票轉向債券；另有19%認為目前殖利率已具吸引力，顯示部分經理人已開始重新評估固定收益資產的配置價值 。
不過，債券整體仍處淨減碼，且經理人對科技股的配置持續升高 ，反映市場尚未全面轉向防禦。後續仍須關注美債殖利率變
化、AI資本支出的投資回報，以及企業獲利能否支撐偏高的市場評價。
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10

台積電財報佳卻未激勵股價，短線面臨總經干擾
• 台積電Q3營收、獲利及EPS同步改寫單季新高：營收年增50.9%、季增17.6%，稅後純益7,630.6億元，年增68.7％、季增8
％；每股稅後純益(EPS)29.43元，整體優於市場預期，但毛利率66.4％較第二季的67.7％下降。
• 財測展望：第四季財測營收預估 523~543億美元，季增上看15％、年增65％，毛利率預估65%~67%，反映高階晶片需求
依然強勁。全年展望方面，預期2026年美元營收年增約44%，較上季預估的「略高於40%」說法更加明確。此外，重申全
年資本支出600~640億美元不變。
• 市場反應：台積電法說會隔日股價小幅下跌，法說會未出現激勵行情，主要原因包含美國通膨升溫壓力、外資連續調節部位，

以及毛利率略為下降。短線籌碼面可能仍會調整，但台積電董事長魏哲家在法說會中強調，第二波AI需求正強勁興起，台積
電正透過多種途徑全力擴充產能，顯示高階晶片前景明確，中長期具投資價值，多家投資機構也紛紛上調目標價。
資料來源：台積電法說會，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.15。
法說會重點整理 說明
AI需求持續升溫
魏哲家坦言，目前仍難以判斷晶圓供需缺口將擴大或縮小，因為
客戶需求持續增加，台積電也正積極提高產能。儘管消費性電子
市場仍受到零組件漲價及總體經濟不確定性影響，但先進製程需
求依然強勁，公司預估2026年全年美元營收將年增約44％。
擴廠進度
(1)台南科學園區新建3奈米廠預計2027年下半年量產，並決定在
台南再增建一座3奈米廠，規畫2028年投入量產。
(2)美國亞利桑那州第二座晶圓廠已完成廠房興建，預計2027年
第三季量產，時程較原先規畫略為提前；日本第二座晶圓廠則將
導入3奈米製程，預計2028年量產。
台積電證實洽談Terafab
合作
魏哲家強調，目前相關合作仍在討論階段，尚未有結論。不過，

無論未來採取何種合作模式，晶圓廠都必須由台積電負責營運。
電力供應疑慮
美國電力供應情況是公司評估AI長期需求的重要因素之一，因此
台積電進一步確認當地電力供應狀況，目前相關研究及評估結果
均良好，尚未發現明顯問題。

• 全球經濟正處於「韌性仍在、風險升高」的階段：國際貨幣基金組織(IMF)指出，AI投資熱潮持續推動美國、台灣等國家成
長，但中東地緣政治衝突、高通膨風險、政府債務問題與AI泡沫化疑慮，也同時為市場埋下不確定因素。
• 市場永遠充滿雜音，定期定額紀律投資更具優勢：本許多人認為定期定額的核心在於「平均成本」，但更重要的是幫助投資
人克服人性。市場上漲時持續投入，可以參與企業獲利成長帶來的成果，市場下跌時持續扣款，則能以較低價格累積更多單
位數。換句話說，定期定額讓投資人不需要每天判斷：明天會不會跌？現在是不是太貴？要不要先停扣觀望？長期而言，紀

律投資往往比短期擇時的成效更佳。
• AI時代更適合定期定額：儘管IMF認為人工智慧發展為全球經濟成長帶來貢獻，但同時也警告，若市場對AI未來獲利能力過
度樂觀，不排除出現股市估值修正。根據歷史經驗，每一次市場重大修正往往也孕育下一輪成長契機，不論AI發展是否出現
泡沫，善用定期定額策略可以在機會與風險之間達到平衡。
韌性仍在、風險升溫，紀律投資更穩健
任一月份定期定額進場投資試算
註：以MSCI全球指數2010/1/31~2025/12/31試算。假設定期定額每月月底投入、
原幣計算。指數資料試算僅供參考，非投資商品之實際績效。資料來源Bloomberg。
短進短出錯失行情的代價大
註：以美國標普500指數為試算範例，期間為1980年~2025年。資料來源Bloomberg。

4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移
民間最終消費支出 民間企業設備 その他民需 公需 外需
（資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」
( )内は年率換算値
(注）2602までは当研究所による改定見込値、2603は予測値
予測

１. 11/16に内閣府から公表される 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、前期比▲0.1％（前期比年
率▲0.3％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になったと推計される。 
２. 高水準の企業収益を背景に設備投資が前期比 1.4％と大幅に増加したが、天候不順の影響
などから民間消費が前期比▲0.3％と減少したこと、原油を中心に輸入が大幅に増加し、
外需が成長率を大きく押し下げたことなどから、小幅ながら 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成
長になったとみられる。 
３. 名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質GDPの伸
びを大きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％、前年比 2.5％と予測する。 
４. 2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時
的な影響が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回
復を妨げる可能性が高い。 
５. 現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むこ
となどから、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまる
と予想している。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-30

2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP 
～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸びを大
きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％（4-6月期：同 1.0％）、前年比 2.5％（4-6月
期：同 2.6％）と予測する。

なお、11/16に内閣府から 2026年 7-9月期の GDP速報が発表される際には、基礎統計の改定や季
節調整のかけ直しなどから、成長率が過去に遡って改定される。当研究所では、2026年 4-6月期の
実質 GDP成長率は、外需、設備投資が上方修正されることから、前期比年率 1.4％から同 2.3％へ
上方修正されると予想している。

2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。

1 10/30までに公表された経済指標をもとに予測している。今後公表される経済指標の結果によって予測値を修正する可
能性がある。

### Page 3

民間消費は前期比▲0.3％と予測する。 
実質賃金はプラスの伸びが続いているが、物価上昇率の高まりによって実質購買力が押し下げら
れていることに加え、台風や豪雨、長雨などの天候不順が個人消費を下押しした。 
2026年 7-9月期の消費関連指標を確認すると、延べ宿泊者数（うち日本人）は前期比 2.8％（4-6
月期：同▲0.9％）の増加となったが、自動車税の
「環境性能割」の廃止を受けて 4-6月期に前期比
10.7％の高い伸びとなった自動車販売台数が、その
反動で同▲7.5％と落ち込んだほか、外食産業売上
高が前期比▲0.3％（4-6月期：同 0.2％）、小売業
販売額指数が前期比▲2.1％（4-6月期：同 0.6％）
と減少した（いずれもニッセイ基礎研究所による
季節調整値、外食産業売上高、小売業販売額指数
は消費者物価指数で実質化）。

・住宅投資～弱めの動きが続く～

住宅投資は前期比▲0.2％と 2四半期連続の減
少を予測する。 
新設住宅着工戸数（季節調整済・年率換算値）
は、建築物省エネ法・建築基準法改正前の駆け
込み需要の反動で、2025年 1-3月期の 86.7万戸
から 4-6月期に 62.0万戸へ急減した後、7-9月
が 71.9万戸と持ち直し、その後は概ね 70万戸
台半ばで推移している。 
GDP統計の住宅投資は住宅価格上昇の影響も
あり弱めの動きとなっている。

・民間設備投資～高水準の企業収益を背景に増加～

民間設備投資は前期比 1.4％と 3四半期ぶりの増加を予測する。 
設備投資の一致指標である資本財総供給は 2026年 4-6月期の前期比 4.9％の後、7-9月期も同 4.9％
となった。また、機械投資の先行指標である機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は 2026年 4-6月
期に前期比 0.2％と 3四半期連続で増加した後、7、8月の平均は 4-6月期を 0.4％上回っている。 
日銀短観 2026年 9月調査では、2026年度の設備投資計画（含むソフトウェア・研究開発投資額、
除く土地投資額）が6月調査から 0.5％上方修正され、前年度比9.3％（全規模・全産業）となった。 
設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報

2026年度

・公的固定資本形成～2四半期連続の減少～

公的固定資本形成は、前期比▲2.4％と予測する。 
公共工事の先行指標である公共工事請負金額
は 2026年 7-9月期に前年比▲2.6％と 6四半期ぶ
りに減少した。また、公共工事の進捗を反映する
公共工事出来高（建設総合統計）は、2026年 4-6
月期に前年比 5.2％と 9四半期連続で増加した後、
2026年 7、8月の平均は同 2.4％と伸びが鈍化し
ている。 
2025年度補正予算による押し上げ効果が弱ま
っていることに加え、2026 年度当初予算の公共
事業関係費の伸びが抑えられていることから、弱
めの動きとなっている。

・外需～4四半期ぶりのマイナス寄与～

外需寄与度は前期比▲0.4％（前期比年率▲1.8％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナスを予測する。財貨・
サービスの輸出が前期比 2.0％、財貨・サービスの輸入が同 4.8％となるだろう。代替調達の進展に
伴う原油輸入量の急増を主因として 輸入が高い伸
びとなり、輸出の伸びを上回ったことから、外需は
成長率の押し下げ要因となった。 
2026年 7-9月期の地域別輸出数量指数を季節調整
値（当研究所による試算値）でみると、米国向けが
前期比 0.9％（4-6月期：同 2.8％） 、EU向けが前期
比▲2.4％（4-6月期：同 0.3％） 、アジア向けが前期
比▲0.3％（4-6 月期：同▲0.8％） 、うち中国向けが

營收走勢圖

研究員/吳大任 TERRY.WU@uni-psg.com

研究部對創意的投資建議維持強力買進，係因:(1)3Q26 營收、毛利
率、營業利益率皆優於預期，(2)4Q26 雖因為 ECB 將送件未給財測，
但客戶對 CPU 需求來看仍持續上修 ，(3)北美車用晶片有望在 2Q27 初
導入 ， 中國車用客戶晶片市佔率持續提升 ， 且持續拓展海外客戶 ，27-
28 年貢獻幅度大幅跳升 ，(4)晶片大廠持續上修 CPU 與加速器的配適
性，更有效率的使用 GPU 之效能，(5)不僅 200 系列晶片，CSP 客戶
的 300 系列晶片創意也有所著墨。綜合以上，上修創意 26 年 EPS 預
估為 63.3 元(原估 54.76 元，+125%YOY)，同步上修 27 年 EPS 預估
為 245.9 元(原估 214.5 元，+288.5%YOY)， 及上修28 年EPS 為 467.7
元(原估 392.6 元，+95.9%YOY，以股本膨脹 3%計算)。考量 27 年下
半年創意的成長動能更加明確上修 ， 且費用控管十分優異 ， 客戶從雲
端 AI 延伸至終端 AI，CPU、加速器、自駕晶片及 CPO 皆有著墨，故
對創意看法更加樂觀，故先前 8600 元目前價到價後，仍維持強力買
進投資建議，目標價調升至 9850 元(27 年 EPS*40XPER OR 28 年
EPS*21XPER)。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營收大幅優於預期，OPM 更加優化：  
創意 3Q26 營收達到創新高且優於上修後的預期的 187.5 億
元，成長35%QOQ，成長117.7%YOY，其中量產營收達到166.7
億元，成長44%QOQ，推估來自於CPU 的貢獻較大，且在2H26
創意也有新增一些產能支援 ， 且NRE 也達到預估的區間上緣 ，
僅衰退 11%QOQ。 毛利率達到20.59%， 優於研究部預期的19%，
更優於市場預估的 19-20%，推估主要是出貨的晶圓良率更加
提升所致。費用控制非常良好，3Q26 費用僅 13.09 億元，低
於 2Q26 的 13.18 億元，但營收卻成長35%QOQ，故使得OPM 提
升至 13.6%，大幅優於先前預估的11%。稅後淨利達到創新高
的 22.73 億元(原估 16.02 億元)，成長 46.1%QOQ，成長
162.1%YOY，EPS 16.96 元(原估 11.96 元)。

2. 因 ECB 將送件，故無 4Q26 財測，但仍正向看待：  
因為創意的 ECB 即將送件，故此次財報後無法給出財測，但
研究部仍認為 4Q26 創意營運動能將上修，主要係因先前在上
半年所提到的投片保有量外，仍有多爭取的 WAFER，從創意
3Q26 每個月的量產營收就可以觀察到持續增加，單月量產營
收已經提升至 65 億元，預估 4Q26 將能持續穩定攀升。另外
NRE 方面，CSP 下一代 CPU 將預估在 4Q26 TAPE OUT，NRE 預

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

估將翻倍成長 。 綜合以上 ，因為量產營收持續優於預期， 故上
修 4Q26 營收為 244.2 億元(原估 236 億元)，成長30.2%QOQ，
成長 97%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為續創新高的 30.1 億元(原
估 25.35 億元)，成長32.5%QOQ，成長159.6%YOY，EPS 22.46
元(原估 18.92 元)。

3. CPU在Agentic AI世代重要性持續提升： 
根據INTEL的資料中心與人工智慧白皮書顯示，CPU 容量不足
可能造成編排卡頓、工具執行延遲、驗證循環變慢，導致GPU
在等待控制平面 （代理編排） 處理完成時利用率不足 。 若要將
代理型工作負載最佳化，就必須增加 CPU 數量，作為控制與
編排層，在兼顧 新式 AI 工作流程分散且多步驟本質的同
時，將資料高效提供給加速器。故根據INTEL實測顯示，在模
型大小為8B規模時 ， 建議加速器與CPU配比為1.4:1， 模型大小
擴大到70B時，建議配比為1:1。故在AMD簡報也顯示，2025-
2030年CPU產值規模將從260億美元提升至2030年的2200億美
元，CAGR達到50%。創意的CSP客戶所ASIC走到第八代後，CPU
與TPU的配置從先前1:4確實提升至1:2，並且全數從先前的
X86 BASE更改成自家研發的ARM BASE CPU。而CSP客戶第八代
產品放量時間點將落在4Q26末，2027年完整貢獻 ， 故從創意量
產營收看出 ， 因為配比提升CSP客戶對CPU需求持續增加 ， 故上
修CPU26年貢獻創意營收從12億美元上修至13-15億美元。在
27年方面，有鑑於客戶對於加速器及CPU的配飾性相對於第七
代提升一倍，加上下一代CPU將在4Q26 TAPE OUT，有望在2H27
開始貢獻。因為二代產品單價提升，故上修CPU貢獻營收程度
從35億美元上修35-40億美元。 
CSP二代CPU將有望在28年完整貢獻，ASP提升幅度較大，且三
代CPU目前已經開始著手研發 ， 故研究部認為 ，CPU對加速器的
配置開始往1:1走後，創意目前在客戶需求延續性強，且持續
有拿到次世代案件下，28年CPU貢獻幅度將達到50%YOY增長。

圖一、INTEL 資料中心白皮書內容

資料來源：INTEL，統一研究部整理

圖二、AMD 對 SERVER CPU 產值的預估

資料來源：AMD，統一研究部整理

車用將成為 27 年開始的新成長支柱 
(1)北美客戶： 
根據供應鏈訪查 ， 北美車用客戶晶片量產時間點將落在2Q26，
創意目前在 5 代晶片進度仍優於競爭對手，故以目前規劃的
投片量來看 ， 研究部維持先前預估貢獻約12 億美金營收不變 。
但根據北美客戶法說，該客戶希望將5 代晶片在 2027 年 4 月
導入機器人使用 ， 故研究部目前僅估算車用的部分 ， 尚未估計
機器人，若該客戶順利量產機器人，則貢獻創意營收有望上
修。 
(2)中國客戶： 
以中國客戶自行揭露的資料來看，全系列征程系列晶片出貨
套數已經高達 1000 萬套，在 26 年 9 月，該客戶的 CEO 在社
群媒體上表示，公司智駕晶片出貨量已達 1500 萬套。另外客
戶給出 26 年征程系列晶片將出貨 500 萬套，27 年將更達到
700 萬套的目標。先前預估 27 年以客戶出貨目標 700 萬套的
50%，350 萬套第 7 代征程晶片做估計，貢獻營收將達到 15-
20 億美元，此預估沒有變化，靜待客戶完成年報公告後再行
調整。

圖三、中國自駕車晶片公司 CEO 微博

資料來源：WEIBO，統一研究部整理

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

兩大車用客戶對創意來說 ，27年僅是開端 ， 首先北美客戶27年
才會導入5代晶片，且是機器人及無人計程車皆會導入，附帶
一提，先前展示的飛天跑車也是搭載5代晶片，故認為在客戶
量產機器人及無人計程車後，晶片需求在 28年將會超過翻倍
成長 。 另外在中國客戶方面， 除了本土市場外 ， 該公司積極拓
展海外市場，7代晶片LIFECYCLE有望比6代更久，28年目前也
以翻倍的狀況預估。

4. CSP客戶三代晶片仍有著墨機會： 
北美另一雲端客戶在10月初表示，200系列加速器已經實現量
產，該客戶已經在各地區DATA CENTER 部屬200系列晶片，而
200系列晶片後段為創意打造，推估LIFE CYCLE出貨量為數十
萬顆 ， 貢獻營收約3-5億美元 。 而在300系列方面 ， 先前市場傳
出主要供應商為美國晶片同業 ， 後來研究部訪查 ， 創意在27年
亦有BOOKING 2nm產品，而300系列晶片由N2P節點打造，故推
估創意亦有部分300系列晶片相關生意，故仍有營收貢獻。而
目前推估400系列晶片有望重回創意，挹注 28年後的營運動
能。

5. 26、27年上修預估，28年持續高度成長： 
創意3Q26營收已經大幅優於預期 ， 加上毛利率擊敗市場預期 ，
另外費用控管得宜 ， 使得3Q26 OPM不減反增 ， 另外4Q26雖然未
給財測，但在產能有所支持加上CPU需求持續推升下，營收也
略有上修，故上修創意26年營收預估為685.2億元(原估648.6
億元)，成長100.7%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為84.8億元(原估
73.28億元)， 成長125%YOY，EPS上修預估為63.3元(原估54.76
元)。 展望27年，CPU貢獻性持續上修 ， 另外也維持車用客戶的
預估值，另外本來就有CSP客戶的200系列加速器開始大幅量
產，300系列目前有所著墨，故上修27年營收預估為2458.5億
元(原估2328億元)，成長258.8%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為
329.5億元(原估287.5億元)， 成長288.5%YOY，EPS上修預估為
245.9元(原估214.5元)。最後，雖然目前28年投片等尚未確
定 ， 不過創意在CSP二代CPU、 中國及北美車用晶片 ， 接續世代
晶片皆有持續獲得新世代案件 ， 另外在CSP ASIC方面 ， 目前有
將從整合封裝能力開始切入 ，CSP 300/400系列晶片皆有斬獲 ，
故研究 部粗估28年創意營收將上修預估為 5145.8億元(原估
4627.5億元)，成長109.3%YOY，稅後淨利預估上修為645.5億
元(原估542億元)，成長95.9%YOY，EPS預估為467.7元(原估
392.6元，以膨脹後3%股本計算)。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

附 錄 二 ： 免 責 宣 言

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

研究部對緯穎(6669)維持買進建議，主係：(1) 3Q26營收季增56%，
大幅優於預期；(2) 4Q26在AWS的ASIC氣冷出貨帶動下，預期4Q營
收季增個位數 ；(3) 雖T3水冷機櫃短期遞延， 但預估27年逐月攀升，
公司仍為主要供應商。(4) AMD MI450 於1H27年放量，客戶與平台
更加多元；(5) 新增之Oracle VR200預期的訂單約2.5K，約在27年
貢獻營收。 綜上所述，研究部上修 2026/2027 年營收至1.46兆元
/3.07兆元(原估1.27兆元/2.38兆元)，EPS上修至143.16元/241.16
元，目前評價僅8~9倍，位於低檔，給予12.5倍本益比，目標價3010
元(2027EPS*12.5PER)。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收大幅優於預期 
緯穎9月營收為1,726億元，月增19.6%，年增100%，整體3Q26
營收達4,346億元 ，季增56.2%，年增63%，營收達成率達到
135%(研究部預估)/128%(市場共識)， 大幅優於預期 ， 主係Meta
通用型及AWS T3氣冷出貨旺盛所致。3Q26財報尚未公布，研究
部上修至毛利率8.9%(原估8.1%)， 季減0.4pts， 優於原先預期 ，
主係通用伺服器占比較高， 產品組合較佳 ，對毛利率形成支撐，
預估3Q EPS約45.56元。

2. 4Q營運動能可望延續 
展望4Q26，雖水冷機櫃須遞延至年底至1H27才會逐漸放量 ，但
仍預期T3氣冷機櫃及通用持續出貨，預估4Q26營收約4,666億
元，季增7.4%，年增59.5%。毛利率8.2% (原估7.3%)，季減
0.7pts， 主係高單價ASIC水冷機櫃占比尚低， 預估EPS約45.18
元。整體而言，2H26在ASIC氣冷及通用伺服器帶動下，營運明
顯優於上半年

3. T3短期雜音不改長期趨勢，2027年水冷逐步放量 
根據供應鏈調查：(1) T3水冷機櫃初期產線磨合不順，客戶因
此下修今年出貨規劃。不過，2027年水冷機櫃出貨規劃仍逐季
攀升，在晶片端的水冷比重也提升 至50~70%，單價為氣冷3-4
倍以上，成為2027年營收成長的主要動能。在AWS下一代T4產
品，公司持續參與機櫃設計，有望採用全水冷設計，預期28年
出貨，為中長期成長動能。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

(2)MI450機櫃部分，Meta專案集中在2027年出貨，單價約450
～500萬美元，訂單逐漸明確，預估約可達5～7K櫃。(3) 新增
Oracle VR200訂單，預期約2.5K櫃，約在27年開始貢獻營收。
公司在GPU伺服器的客戶版圖進一步擴大。

4. 目前評價位於低檔，維持買進建議 
綜上所述，考量各客戶訂單逐漸明確， 研究部上修2026/2027
年營收至1.46兆元/3.07兆元(原估1.27兆元/2.38兆元)，2027
年毛利率雖然因高單價水冷機櫃及GPU機櫃比重提升，下滑至
6.5%(原估7.1%)， 但營收規模大幅擴張 ， 獲利仍可望顯著成長 。
股本部分，緯穎配發股票股利，除權基準日為9/8，配股率達
1.9828倍，股本由18.69億元增至56.02億元，EPS因此依比例
稀釋，故2026/2027年稀釋後EPS上修至143.16元/241.16元。
目前評價僅8~9倍，位於低檔，給予12倍本益比，目標價3010
元(2027EPS*12.5PER)。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下

3Q26Pre: 환율 하락에도 성장 흐름 유지 
매출액 1조 3,477억원(+7.2%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 5,957억원(-6.0%, OPM 
44.2%)로 컨센서스에 부합할 것으로 전망. 3분기 원달러 평균 환율은 1,423원으로 전분기 
평균 환율 대비 약 5.2% 하락에도 불구하고 성장 흐름 지속. 
2분기 노조 파업으로 인한 영향은 3분기 생산을 진행했으며 4분기 매출에 반영될 예정으로 
연간 실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상. GSK로부터 인수한 록빌 공장도 안정적
으로 생산이 이루어지고 있으며 CAPA 여유분은 추가 수주를 진행 중으로 28~29년 이후 
완전 가동 전망. 
4Q, 6공장 건설과 폴리펩타이드 인수 
유상 증자 관련 투자 설명서 상 투자 계획에 따르면 6공장 투자는 26년부터 29년까지 진행 
될 예정으로 29년 가동 목표. 6공장은 5공장과 동일한 18만 리터 규모로 건설되며, 연내 투
자 집행을 시작함에 따라 건설 발표도 임박한 것으로 추정. 동사는 일정 수준 이상 기존 공
장 수주를 확보한 후 후속 공장 건설을 진행해오고 있어 non-binding 수주 계약의 본계약 
전환도 순차적으로 이루어질 것으로 전망. 
폴리펩타이드 인수도 현재 공개 매수를 진행 중에 있으며 연내 마무리될 것으로 예상. 
1H26 폴리펩타이드 매출은 2.37억 유로(+41.6%)로 GLP-1 agonist 등 펩타이드 의약품 
수요 증가에 따라 높은 성장 지속. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원으로 하향 
매수 의견을 유지하며 목표 주가는 기존 210만원에서 200만원으로 하향. 목표 주가 하향은 
유상 증자에 따른 EPS 희석에 기인. 글로벌 항체 CDMO 수요가 여전히 견조하며, 폴리펩
타이드 인수로 펩타이드로의 확장에 따른 추가적인 성장 기대. 주주 대상 유상 증자 영향과 
파업 우려 등이 있으나 노사 합의 시 주가 반등 전망.

### Page 1

瑞银的 Mark Paski ，剔除 AI 股的标普 500 成分股本⽉有望上涨 6% ，为 2022 ⾄ 2023 年 AI 交
易开始升温以来的最佳⽉度表现。随着利率⾛低、企业盈利保持韧性，上涨正从少数消费
⻰头扩散到更多股票。
摘要：
周五早盘， AI 股再遭重挫，纳斯达克 100 指数下跌 3% ，等权重标普 500 指数却逆势上涨约
1.2% 。指数之下的分化表明，跌幅⾼度集中在⼤型科技股和 AI 相关股票上。
标普 500 指数当时下跌 1.5% 。英特尔录得有记录以来最⼤⽇内跌幅， AI 主导的抛售让本周的科技
股跌势进⼀步加深；⾼盛追踪美国上市 AI 概念股的 GS Broad AI US Index 下跌 4.7% 。
防御板块则受到追捧。必需消费是标普 500 表现最好的板块，上涨 1.64% ，板块指数在纽约午盘
前⼀度创下纪录新⾼。
财报撑起必需消费
标普 1500 必需消费指数盘中最多上涨 2.3% ，临近美东时间 11 点回吐部分涨幅。领涨的是两家刚
交出亮眼财报的公司。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⾼露洁 - 棕榄盘前⼀度上涨 5.5% ，开盘后涨约 2.5% 。这家家庭及个⼈护理⽤品公司的利润和总销
售额均超过彭博⼀致预期。尽管美国及北美市场表现偏软，但已在改善，强劲的利润和业绩指
引占了上⻛。
零⻝⽣产商亿滋国际盘前涨幅略超 7% ，午盘前上涨约 3% 。公司第三季度业绩超出预期，主要靠
稳健的销量增⻓。
剔除 AI 股，标普 500 本⽉有望涨 6%
瑞银的 Mark Paski 表⽰，剔除 AI 股的标普 500 成分股本⽉有望上涨 6% ，为 2022 ⾄ 2023 年 AI 交易
开始升温以来的最佳⽉度表现。
他认为，随着利率⾛低、企业盈利保持韧性，上涨正从少数⻰头扩散到更多股票。第三季度财
报季初期， 78% 的公司每股收益超出市场预期，但利润率仍是消费类公司的关键问题。
可选消费公司对需求的判断也偏乐观。希尔顿预计，旗下酒店体系的增⻓势头将延续⾄今年余
下时间并进⼊ 2027 年；皇家加勒⽐称， 2027 年的预订进度领先于历史⽔平，价格也更⾼；捷蓝
航空则表⽰， 9 ⽉的强劲表现延续到了 10 ⽉。 Paski 提醒，更⼤的不确定性仍在利润和利润率。

“ 如果市场⼴度继续改善，除了近期的领涨股，基本⾯更强的消费类股票也会越来越具备配置价
值。 ” 他写道。
从周五的盘⾯看，投资者是在把资⾦从 AI 交易挪向防御类股票，并没有恐慌性地全⾯撤离股市。
即便 AI 相关交易遭到重创，普通个股依然站得住。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Document 306: Bank of England set for first rate hike as energy shock worsens

Source PDF: all report pdf/Bank of England set for first rate hike as energy shock worsens.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

We expect a 7-2 vote to hike rates this week and, barring a
collapse in energy prices, we think a follow-up move in
February is likely too. However, market pricing for the Bank of
England still looks extreme

James Smith, Michiel Tukker and Chris Turner
The Bank of England is set to raise rates by 25bp to 4% on Thursday
Higher energy prices set to push inﬂation
above 4%
The Bank of England has so far managed to avoid a rate hike since
the start of the Iran war, but that’s about to change. We, like most,
expect a hike at its 5 November meeting, and think a follow-up
move in February is more likely than not.
Why now? Until now, the Bank would argue it’s been on “active
hold”. It was set to cut rates by upwards of 50 basis points this year,
so simply not doing that amounted to de facto tightening. By that
logic, the Bank has added a similar amount of policy restriction as
the European Central Bank, which has lifted its deposit rate twice.

### Page 2

But the worsening energy crisis has made it much harder for the
Bank not to act. The spike in natural gas prices means inﬂation is
set to take another leg higher into winter. A circa 25% rise in the
regulated cap on household energy bills in January is all but baked
in.
We expect headline CPI to peak a touch above 4% early next year,
and we think the BoE's new forecasts will show this too. The Bank’s
July projections had inﬂation reaching 3.2% in late 2026, based on

oil below US$80/bbl and natural gas at around half its current level.
Energy prices have risen signiﬁcantly since
the Bank's July forecasts
*November forecasts based on a three week observation window until 27
October, which is what we assume the BoE will use for its forecasts
Source: Macrobond, ING
The Bank is set to hike rates for insurance
purposes
4% is an important line in the sand for the BoE. Last summer, the
Bank set out research suggesting that second-round eﬀects

become more likely once inﬂation surpasses this level. We suspect
this is still front of mind for many oﬃcials.
Then again, there is essentially no sign of these much-feared
second-round eﬀects so far. Away from motor fuel, inﬂation has
been in line with the Bank’s July forecasts. Our measure of 'energy-

### Page 3

intensive' inﬂation has actually fallen this year, even after
accounting for one-oﬀ changes in water bills and car tax.
One of the Bank’s favoured measures of ‘core services’ inﬂation,
which strips out volatile and indexed categories, is well-behaved
too. And though it has likely bottomed out, private-sector wage
growth at 3.4% year-on-year is hardly problematic. Despite signs of
stabilisation in the jobs market, there’s little evidence wage
pressures are set to build.

True, growth has been much better than feared. But then the Bank
has often taken a more sceptical view of the GDP numbers,
preferring to look at more benign survey-based measures of
activity.
This all leaves the Bank in a strange situation: hiking not because
the economic data is telling it to, but because of the risks presented
by sharply higher energy prices. The energy crisis has reached
suﬃcient depth and longevity that oﬃcials likely feel they,

reluctantly, have to act.
'Energy-intensive' inﬂation hasn't picked up
Based on data from the ONS categorising the inﬂation basket by energy
intensity
Source: Macrobond, ING
The key things to watch
So what should we watch at the November meeting?

### Page 4

First, the vote split. We know there are two doves, Swati Dhingra and
Alan Taylor, who are deeply sceptical of the need for higher rates.
But we suspect the other seven committee members will back a
hike. Could there be more dissenters? Governor Andrew Bailey
hasn’t sounded hugely enthusiastic about hiking, though a
governor has only dissented twice since 1997 and both times in the
ﬁnancial crisis.
So if there is extra dissent, Sarah Breeden looks the most likely

candidate, having stressed that most businesses are absorbing the
energy shock rather than raising prices. Dave Ramsden is another,
less likely, candidate.
Second, there’s the forward guidance. We think the Bank will be as
non-committal as possible. Something like: “The Committee will
carefully assess the extent to which higher energy prices are
propagating into wage and price-setting. The timing and pace of
any further increases will depend on the evolution of the inﬂation

outlook. Monetary policy is not on a pre-set path.”
That’s a clever way of saying: we could hike again, but it’s not
inevitable.
If there’s going to be a hawkish surprise, it would be if the Bank says
something like “some further tightening in monetary policy is likely
to be appropriate over the coming months”.
Another rate hike in February seems to be the growing consensus –
and our own view too. There’s little precedent for ‘one-and-done’
rate rises. But it’s one thing thinking that, another saying it out

loud. There’s a lot that can happen between now and February. And
having made a hawkish misstep all the way back in March, when
the Bank sounded open-minded about tighter policy, opening the
ﬂoodgates for markets to price in rate hikes, it’ll want to tread
carefully here.
A more dovish formulation would be if the Bank makes further hikes
explicitly contingent on second-round eﬀects: one hike as an
insurance move, but anything more requiring solid data. For

example, “If there were evidence of more persistent second-round

eﬀects in wage and price-setting, further tightening in monetary
policy would be required”.
Finally, watch the individual member paragraphs tucked away in
the minutes. Does anyone formally push back against market
pricing, which has more than four hikes priced by this time next
year? Even Chief Economist Huw Pill, one of the hawks, has recently
indicated that such pricing looks unlikely.
The impact on rates markets
Even if the Bank does choose to push back, will markets listen?

A simple model of two-year swap rates against oil, natural gas,
equity markets and the equivalent USD and EUR rates shows that
these global factors are explaining less of the daily movement in
sterling rates than they were over the summer.
Global factors are explaining less of GBP
rates moves
Source: Macrobond, ING
Indeed, the dovish repricing that we’ve seen for the Federal Reserve
and European Central Bank has not been mirrored in the UK.
Perhaps it’s because Britain lacks a story like France has or, in

America’s case, AI fears. But robust UK growth data may also be
playing a role. Economic surprise indices have been touching multi-
year highs.

Still, UK swap rates remain deeply correlated with oil prices. And
barring a big surprise, and with more than 100bp of hikes already
priced, it’s hard to see the Bank triggering a substantial repricing of
rate expectations at this meeting.
GBP rates remain positively correlated with
oil - unlike EUR rates
Source: Macrobond, ING
The impact on the pound
Sterling is trading reasonably well, helped by the view that high
energy prices leave the Bank of England no alternative but to hike

rates on Thursday.
Upside economic surprises, UK ﬁscal policy seemingly heading in
the right direction and warmer ties with the EU all seem to be
helping sterling too. Notably, speculators are still running
reasonably large short positions in sterling, which suggests this
period of sterling outperformance can continue.
EUR/GBP has come a long way very quickly – a near 4% drop in a
little over a month. Some consolidation is probably due. But with
high energy prices likely to keep expectations of higher UK policy

rates in place until early next year and French budgetary problems
unresolved, it is too early to expect a signiﬁcant low in EUR/GBP.

Document 380: Is AI Useless_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Is AI Useless_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Is AI Useless?
Date:    15 minutes ago
Is AI Useless?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Is AI Useless?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 26 Oct 2026
View in browser

I. AI's Toxic Financial Trifecta
Recent AI jitters have been fueled by a potentially toxic trifecta of financial
constraints: elevated borrowing costs, uncertain investment returns, and
concerns about whether sufficient capital exists to finance everyone's
ambitious AI spending plans. Consider the following:
(1) Elevated borrowing costs. The 10-year Treasury yield is hovering
near the upper end of its "old normal" range of 4.0%-5.0% (chart). That
puts it at the highest level since 2007. Overall, credit spreads (the extra

### Page 2

yield paid over equivalent US Treasuries, such as the 10-year note) have
not blown out uniformly across the board. Instead, the market has
bifurcated sharply between the pristine balance sheets of mega-cap tech
companies and the leveraged balance sheets of pure-play
infrastructure/data center borrowers.
Yields on AI debt have not broadly surged relative to Treasuries for the
dominant Big Tech issuers, whose balance sheets continue to be treated
virtually like "proxy sovereigns." However, the credit market is increasingly

imposing credit discipline, demanding significantly higher risk premiums
and spreads from the leveraged second- and third-tier infrastructure
borrowers that are building out the physical plumbing of AI. Increased
concerns about the credit quality of AI borrowers have been reflected in
widening credit default spreads (chart).

### Page 3

(2) Uncertain returns. Gartner estimates that AI data center operators
must generate $8.5 trillion in cumulative revenues through 2030 to cover
their investments and associated financing costs and still achieve a 12%
return on invested capital. Yet Gartner projects no more than $3.6 trillion in
attributable AI revenues over that period. In our view, if the numbers don't
add up, the pace of the AI buildout will slow. That wouldn’t be a calamity.
(3) Increasingly constrained capital. Meta's Q3 earnings report

intensified concerns about capital availability. Despite strong revenue
growth, the company reported negative free cash flow for the first time
since 2012 and announced new non-voting shares to facilitate fundraising,
warning of an "increasingly difficult fundraising environment." Today, Meta
unveiled plans to raise up to $95 billion through equity offerings.
Meanwhile, Broadcom CEO Hock Tan identified capital as potentially the
biggest constraint on AI development. BlackRock CEO Larry Fink

described competition for capital as the fiercest of his career, while Nvidia
CEO Jensen Huang observed that neocloud demand is "off the charts,"
but capital is scarce.
Today, Elon Musk warned that more than 200 specialized AI cloud
providers (i.e., neoclouds) are headed for a wave of "guppy-level death
and consolidation." Musk stated that debt markets have "almost entirely
shut off access to money for any company that doesn't have substantial
revenue independent of AI data centers." In recent weeks, the neocloud

providers’ stock prices have tumbled (chart).

### Page 4

On balance, hyperscalers’ stock prices have held up relatively well (chart).
II. Will AI Pay Off?
Demand for compute nevertheless remains extraordinary. Global
spending on cloud infrastructure services surged 43% y/y to $143 billion in
Q2, its fastest growth in eight years. But how sustainable is that demand
growth? Ultimately, the marginal economic benefits derived from AI must
justify the marginal cost of supplying the necessary compute.
We believe AI is already generating tangible economic benefits, even if

they have yet to become clearly visible in aggregate macroeconomic
statistics. Consider the following:
(1) Anthropic's S-1. Anthropic’s IPO filing today offered five revealing
takeaways: First, revenues soared from $1.2 billion in H1-2025 to $16.3
billion in H1-2026. Second, operating losses narrowed from $2.1 billion to
$693 million, with Q2 marking the company's first quarterly operating profit
of $974 million. Third, revenue per dollar of compute expense surged 5.2-

fold to $2.08 between Q1-2025 and Q2-2026. Fourth, Anthropic held
$131.7 billion in cash and short-term investments as of June, alongside
$3.9 billion in H1 operating cash flow. Fifth, its enterprise footprint
expanded rapidly, with more than 11,900 customers spending at least
$100,000 annually as of September, up from roughly 950 a year earlier.
(2) Corporate America's emerging AI payoffs. An August Janus
Henderson study of 109 companies found that 69% reported some margin

### Page 5

improvement associated with productivity gains and AI-related cost
reductions. Among the examples cited, Shell reported $400 million in
annual savings and a 45% reduction in unplanned downtime; Freeport-
McMoRan increased mill throughput 10% while cutting unit costs by up to
15%; inventories at one Hitachi facility fell 50% and lead times shortened
by 77%; and IBM reported $4.5 billion in AI-related savings in 2025.
Last Thursday, Barclays announced plans to eliminate approximately

5,000 jobs, or 5% of its workforce, as AI and broader technological
modernization improve efficiency. PayPal is targeting $1.5 billion in
annualized savings, partly through AI adoption.
(3) Corporate profit margins. Corporate America is becoming more
profitable. The forward profit margin of the S&P 500 excluding its
Information Technology sector has been climbing this year, reaching
12.9% in October (chart). Excluding just the Semiconductors industry, the

forward profit margin rose to a record 14.4%. The forward profit margin in
Semiconductors rose to a record 51.8% in the week of October 23. But
most of the industry’s margin expansion since 2024 is attributable to
capacity constraints pushing prices higher.
A closer look reveals encouraging trends in industries where AI
applications could be particularly beneficial. Forward profit margins have
expanded in numerous financial industries where AI offers significant

opportunities for automation (chart).

### Page 6

Health Care stands to benefit from AI applications that can streamline
clinical workflows and administrative processes (chart).
(4) Bottom line. We aren't yet seeing significant AI-driven profit margin
expansion across the economy, but anecdotal evidence of AI-related
productivity gains is mounting. We expect AI adoption to become
increasingly widespread across industries, eventually delivering
substantial productivity growth and boosting corporate profit margins. AI's

economic payoff may not yet be obvious in aggregate statistics, but that
doesn't mean it isn't already materializing.
💡
Join the discussion with Dr. Ed below! Paid members can now
leave comments or questions at the end of our QuickTakes, and it

### Page 7

clause.

Style Exposure
We are incrementally positive on Jentech with multiple catalysts ahead. Near-term,

we believe the company’s 3Q26 earnings could be the first driver with a potential
GM beat, which should be reported in the second week of Nov. Longer-term, we
believe the drivers are from the incremental adoption of the removable lid in some
Nvidia Rubin GPUs in 2027 (in addition to Rubin Ultra), followed by the
meaningful Google TPU v9 spec upgrade potential in 2028 as another multi-fold
content increase story (based on JPMe), along with a structural margin expansion

trend given continued GPU and ASIC upgrades. Thus, we revise up our 2027E and
2028E EPS materially and are now 28% and 18% above the current BBGe
consensus.
• 3Q26 preview: expecting a stronger GM growth; 4Q26 outlook: slightly
better rev growth. We revise up our 3Q26 GM to 50.1% (vs current BBGe
consensus at 48.7%), which implies another quarter of strong GM expansion
(vs 2Q/1Q26 GM at 46.8%/41.8%), driven by better product mix to more AI
server business. Reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$9.5bn was largely in line with

the company’s guidance, and the 30% QoQ growth was mainly driven by the
decent ramp of Rubin GPU. As the Rubin 1-piece lid shipment should remain
at high volume into 4Q26, we believe the company’s revenue momentum will
also continue, with incremental growth from small volume of removable lid
shipment. Hence, we are now modeling Jentech’s 4Q26 revenue to grow 3%
QoQ, with GM further expanding thanks to ongoing mix enhancement.
• Further 2027 upside from incremental Rubin removable lid adoption;

2028 another earnings level-up year driven by meaningful upgrade in
TPU v9.We revise up our 2027E and 2028E revenue estimates materially by
22% and 14%, which are now 24% and 16% above consensus, respectively. For
the 2027 upward revision, it mainly reflects the incremental adoption of
removable lid (3-piece lid) in partial Rubin GPU from the next 1-2 quarter, in
addition to Rubin Ultra, as long as the company can ramp up its yield and
production of removable lid in the next few months with on-schedule capacity

expansion (new Taoyuan Aerotropolis plant) and equipment move-in (e.g. the
in-house screw locking equipment). More encouragingly, we believe 2028 will
see even greater earnings upside with Google TPU v9 likely upgrading its heat
spreader solution significantly to 2-piece or 3-piece lid design, in our view, as
the potential chip size (4-die) and TDP (potentially 2k+W or even higher)
should be a big level-up compared to v8, which makes the current stiffener-

only solution insufficient. As the content of 2-piece or 3-piece lid solution
could be 5-10x higher than the v8 stiffener, Google TPU v9 (with meaningful
volume) could drive the next wave of the earnings upgrade cycle for Jentech
from 2028 (following Nvidia Rubin/Rubin Ultra as the major driver in 2027).
• Implications. We lift our 2027E and 2028E EPS by 28% and 17% to NT$193
and NT$295, respectively, reflecting the incremental removable lid adoption

Summary Investment Thesis and Valuation
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component
maker worldwide, with leading production capabilities across
heat spreader products (including stiffener and all kinds of lids).
We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-
28, driven by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI

chips and increasing TAM into new solutions (e.g., CPO switch
ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server
sockets for chip customers. We believe Jentech’s technology
leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings
power in the long term.
Valuation
Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which

is largely in line with Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the
AI server boom started.

Investment Thesis, Valuation and Risks
Jentech Precision Industrial Co. (Overweight; Price Target: NT
$8,800.00)
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component maker worldwide, with
leading production capabilities across heat spreader products (including stiffener and all
kinds of lids). We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-28, driven

by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI chips and increasing TAM into new
solutions (e.g. CPO switch ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server sockets for chip customers. We
believe Jentech’s technology leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings power in the long term.
Valuation

Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which is largely in line with
Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the AI server boom started.
Risks to Rating and Price Target
Downside risks to our rating and price target include: 1) Potential delay in mainstream 3-
piece/removable lid design adoption, and 2) market share loss from competition.

### Page 1

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Following on?
• We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps
to 3% at the 28 October Review.
• A vote seems very possible as differences in
view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook
has really shifted in recent weeks.
• Data since September show a higher outlook
for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP
starting point, but increased risks to domestic
consumption and residential investment. On

balance, the medium-term inflation picture
will probably be judged to have worsened
slightly given recent movements in energy
prices and the exchange rate.
• Forward guidance will likely leave open
the option to raise the OCR further at the
December Monetary Policy Statement without
presenting it as a sure thing.
• Given the ‘pause’ signal in September, the
press release will likely emphasise the more
concerning short-term inflation picture driven

by the lower exchange rate and higher energy
prices. The tightening in financial conditions
through higher short- and long-term interest
rates, as well as lingering spare capacity, will
be presented as offsets that means the MPC
retains confidence that inflation will return
towards 2% over time.
• We still think a more measured tightening
approach is appropriate from here. Raising the
OCR at every meeting until something ‘breaks’
could be a risky strategy if downside growth

concerns crystalise.
• Nonetheless, the expected hike will bring the
OCR to the top of the RBNZ’s neutral range
more quickly. We now think the OCR will hit
3.5% a meeting earlier in February. Thereafter
we expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, lifting the OCR to a
peak of 4%.

RBNZ decision and communication.
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. As there will likely be differences
in perspective about recent developments in energy
prices and the exchange rate, we think it is quite possible
that the decision will be reached after a formal vote,
rather than by consensus.
As the RBNZ will not be presenting new forecasts (aside

perhaps from updated short-term CPI forecasts) we will
not see an updated OCR forecast track. Rather we expect
the RBNZ will aim to leave their options open at the next
meeting in December. As in September, we expect the
RBNZ to note that conditional on the central economic
22 Oct 2026 | Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of RBNZ October 2026
Monetary Policy Review

(Wednesday 28 October, NZT 2pm).

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
outlook, the OCR may need to increase further (the
September projection had portrayed an OCR eventually
rising to 3.25%). The RBNZ will emphasise the future OCR
path is not predetermined.
In September, the MPC agreed that further tightening in
policy was likely required. The bulk of the MPC saw upside
risks to the inflation outlook while two members (Conway
and Hansen) thought the risks were balanced. Recent
developments will likely not have made those seeing

upside risks to inflation more comfortable, while the
more dovish MPC members will likely still see the growth
downside risks given continued softness in house prices
and the mounting pressure on real incomes. Indeed, all
MPC members saw some degree of downside risks to
growth which likely remain relevant. All these messages
should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC will likely continue to present their decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July, where

a higher OCR now is insurance against a much higher OCR
later should upside medium-term inflation risks arise. We
doubt the objective will be to push market interest rates
materially higher.
The exchange rate will likely have greater prominence
at this Review, as it did at the July meeting. The TWI is
just over 3% lower than previously assumed which – if
sustained – will add some inflation pressure over the
next few quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted

the RBNZ’s medium term outlook for inflation. So, the
discussion will seek to link the implications of the lower
exchange rate for short term inflation projections with the
risk this spills over into medium term inflation. We doubt
the RBNZ will be trying to actively support the exchange
rate in any meaningful way, and indeed the lower
exchange rate may be presented as something supportive
of the medium-term growth profile, reducing risks there.
Financial conditions more broadly are likely to receive

some attention. While the exchange rate is lower than
assumed, there have also been marked increases in
wholesale long-term interest rates and (to a lesser extent)
mortgage rates. The impact of higher interest rates on
managing medium term inflation risks will likely be noted.
The RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that at this
stage there is little evidence of second-round inflation
impacts coming from the Iran war and the associated rise
in energy prices. We note that most other central banks

are drawing a similar judgement at this point. Today’s CPI
might be consistent with that judgement, although core
inflation measures have increased and will likely be noted
by the more hawkish members of the MPC. We expect the
RBNZ to point to the risks that such persistent inflation
pressures might emerge at some point. But that would
depend on the duration of the oil price shock. We expect
the RBNZ to continue to have the assumption that the
shock dissipates and that energy prices and hence CPI

inflation will be appreciably lower in the medium term.

RBNZ target range
We expect the RBNZ to note the labour market remains
cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Still-weak employment growth compared to labour force

growth, weak job vacancies growth and low levels of
labour market turnover are in line with this view and could
be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge a still-solid
global growth outlook but with significant downside risks
looking forward given the Iran war and concerns around
the sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Implications for Westpac’s forecast profile
for the OCR.
The expected hike will bring the OCR to the top of the

range of the RBNZ’s neutral rate estimates more quickly.
It seems likely that the OCR will hit 3.5% a meeting earlier
now. Beyond the pause that we had previously expected
at next week’s meeting, we had expected the RBNZ would
be looking to slow the pace of OCR increases once it had
reached 3.5%. We retain the latter expectation, so this
earlier hike also implies that the pause will come a little
earlier, after two further 25bp hikes at the December

2026 and February 2027 reviews.
We continue to see further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings – taking the OCR to a peak
of 4.0% – as it’s likely that more restrictive conditions
will be required to push core inflation back sustainably
to 2%. Needless to say, the path of the OCR in 2027 will
depend on the evolution of numerous factors that remain
very uncertain, not least being developments in the
Middle East.
Arguments in favour of a hike.

• The ongoing conflict in the Middle East and related
developments in global energy markets point to upside
risk for headline inflation, at least in the near term.
Westpac’s forecasts for headline inflation are now
at 2.5% or above for most of 2027 now. Upside risks
to core inflation and inflation expectations also may
emerge the longer the oil price remains above $90-
100/bbl.

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• The exchange rate is notably weaker in recent months
as global interest rates have been rising, and the NZ
OCR is low compared to Australian and US settings.
Financial markets even envisage further interest rate
increases in Europe given the high levels of inflation
implied by their September adverse scenarios (3.2%
2027 HICP inflation vs 2.5% baseline). If the NZ OCR
doesn’t keep pace with global trends, then the

risk is for more inflation generated by a lower NZD
exchange rate.
• The ‘real’ OCR rate is relatively low – plausibly around
-1 to -2 % based on headline inflation projections.
Based on core CPI levels the real policy rate is likely
negative. An OCR in the 3.25-3.5% range would deliver
positive real rates based on core CPI forecasts.
• The economy has grown above trend in the last year
reflecting strong export sector incomes and the
lagged impact of past low OCR settings. The output

gap is likely still negative but is closing. Relying on a
prolonged negative output gap to bring core inflation
pressures down could be risky.
• The areas of economic strength in the last year are not
yet waning. Agriculture sector returns remain strong,
while global growth forecasts are being revised up as
the AI investment boom proceeds. The construction
sector is picking up, as is the manufacturing sector.
Tourism is strong, supported by an advantageous

exchange rate.
• Market pricing leans strongly towards an OCR hike at
this meeting. It may be less disruptive to go along with
market pricing, especially given the RBNZ’s forecasts
imply two further OCR increases including one by
year-end. Moving now versus six weeks later is of little
consequence from a macro view. A smaller ‘splash’ at
this meeting could be desirable given the proximity to
the election.
Arguments in favour of no change.
• For all the upward pressure on short term inflation,

there is little evidence that the medium-term inflation
outlook has shifted much. Domestic inflation in the
September quarter was 0.3ppts weaker than the RBNZ
had expected, which should be a source of comfort.
In addition, energy and exchange rate inflation
impacts on inflation occur too early for the MPC to
offset and will be what they will be. And measures of
pricing behaviour and inflation expectations in recent
consumer and business surveys seem benign given the

scale of recent energy price increases.
• Firms report pressure on margins and an inability
to set prices high enough to recoup cost increases.
Profitability is struggling which clouds the outlook.
Firms expect better times ahead – perhaps because
they think energy prices will fall – but note tougher
trading conditions now. Hiring still seems modest
albeit positive. It does not appear that hiring is yet
strong enough to reduce the high unemployment rate.

• Housing market trends look quite weak and seem
set to constrain consumer spending and residential
investment. Despite very low mortgage rates in the
last year, house sales have declined and house prices
are flat to falling. It seems unlikely we will see a change
in these weak trends soon – especially if interest
rates continue to rise. The result is we are not seeing
a ‘consumption rich’ recovery and growth is relatively
unbalanced. There could be additional risks to the

sustainability of this recovery should external demand
suddenly weaken for some reason.
How might the election play into the
decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not
be politically motivated. However, the election outcome
is close. And should it result in a Labour Party-led
coalition, it seems likely the new government would adopt
the Labour Party’s promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual

mandate. That would require the MPC to weigh the degree
of labour market slack more highly in its deliberations. As
the degree of labour market slack currently seems high,
a new mandate might justify the MPC taking more time
to return inflation to the middle of the target range (our
earlier note discusses this issue). This could figure into
the deliberations of some MPC members if the decision
between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it now is

viewed as finely balanced.
Scenarios.
Our core scenario, with perhaps around a 60%
probability, is that the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp
and signals the December meeting is live but without
committing to a markedly tighter future stance than
presented in September. Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the OCR increase to 3% and have the RBNZ more
strongly emphasise the upside risks to core and

medium-term inflation, leading to the implication that
an upward revision of the RBNZ’s forward OCR profile
is in the offing in December. The downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) is one
where the RBNZ leaves the OCR unchanged and signals
that the medium-term outlook for inflation has not
yet changed meaningfully relative to that forecast
in the September meeting, despite the higher near-

term inflation outlook. This would cause the RBNZ to
reiterate its previous forecast, i.e. one 25bp increase in
December and one more hike in 2027 sometime.

### Page 4

4
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Appendix: Key developments since the
September Monetary Policy Statement.
Activity: The economy is coming from a stronger starting
point than the RBNZ assumed in September. GDP rose
by 0.2% in the June quarter, and along with revisions to
earlier quarters, the annual growth rate lifted to 2.6%
against the RBNZ’s forecast of 2.1%. Against this, recent
high-frequency data and the resurgence in fuel prices
presents some downside risk to the RBNZ’s growth

forecasts for the second half of the year. The RBNZ’s
Nowcast estimates of growth have drifted lower in recent
weeks, and while these are not the same as the RBNZ’s
official forecasts, they may be a useful guide as to the
direction of change.
Sentiment-based activity indicators: The NZIER’s
Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion was mixed. Firms
were more optimistic about the coming months, but
recent performance has been more subdued and
continues to fall short of expectations. Hiring and

investment intentions picked up compared to last
quarter, but more firms cited a lack of demand rather
than capacity as their main constraint on growth.
Meanwhile, the monthly PMI surveys have shown ongoing
strength in manufacturing, with more modestly positive
readings for service industries.
Housing market: Housing market conditions have
remained soft. House sales have fallen a further 4%
since July, the average time to sell remains elevated,
and prices have continued to edge lower. The RBNZ had

already assumed a modest decline in prices over 2026,
and the recent performance has been broadly in line with
their assumption.
Labour market: Filled jobs have picked up in the last
two months, having been flat through the first half of this
year. That’s in line with the RBNZ’s forecast of a rise in
employment and a tick down in the unemployment rate
to 5.5% in the September quarter. The QSBO showed
some tightening in the availability of skilled workers and a

lift in hiring intentions. Net migration has been in line with
the RBNZ’s assumptions.
Inflation: Annual inflation slowed to 3.7% in the
September quarter, below the RBNZ’s forecast of
3.9%. The downside surprise was on non-tradables
inflation, while tradables inflation was in line with the
RBNZ’s forecast. Underlying inflation pressures remain
elevated, with the various measures of core inflation
close to or above the top of the RBNZ’s 1-3% target

range. Since the September MPS forecasts were finalised,
oil prices have surged higher again, with petrol prices
setting new record highs. The Dubai crude spot price
is currently around $110/bbl, compared to the RBNZ’s
assumption of an average price of around $84/bbl for the
December quarter.
Inflation expectations: The QSBO showed that firms’
expectations of costs and prices remain elevated, but
lower than they were in the June quarter. ANZ’s monthly

survey showed a small dip in inflation expectations
among both businesses and consumers, although the
survey period may have pre-dated the worst of the
latest resurgence in fuel prices (the next updates will be
released just after the OCR review).
Trade: The June quarter terms of trade were in line with
the RBNZ’s forecast. Prices for New Zealand’s commodity
exports have generally remained firm in recent months,
with a pickup in dairy prices against some easing in beef

and lamb prices.
Financial conditions: Domestic financial conditions have
provided mixed signals since the September MPS. Long-
term interest rates have risen sharply along with global
markets, which the RBNZ may regard as an unwarranted
tightening of financial conditions to the extent that it
hasn’t reflected domestic economic conditions. Most
fixed-term mortgage rates have risen by 5-15 basis points
since the September MPS, but rates for the popular

two-year term have actually fallen a little as lenders
have looked to compete in this space. Meanwhile, the
New Zealand dollar has fallen sharply and the trade-
weighted index is just over 3% below the RBNZ’s assumed
average for the December quarter. If sustained, this will
add to imported inflation pressures over the year ahead.

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 27) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027 , as AI-led expansion continues.  The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4 %, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2. 3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026 , but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risk s 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures ( PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees c ore PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2. 4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates  at 4.0% , after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8 
ECB to hike once more  in December, then pause . The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5%  y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer -term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy , and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year . Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets , which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non -manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD  133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though , with  technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key  rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first -home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local -government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a  quarterly pace  until Q2 2027.  Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4% , and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five -year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3%  in Q1 2027 . Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle  in H2 2027. Two government -appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an  Underweight view on Developed Market ( DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data . With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps  hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3 -7-year part of the  US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We remain Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, 
government bond yields have retraced meaningfully after a 
sharp bear steepening through September and early October. 
We expect real yield s to stay elevated over the next six 
months as AI capex and productivity growth continue to drive 
above-trend economic growth. We expect two more 25bps 
rate hikes by June 2027.  
Over the next few months, we expect US government bond 
yields to remain rangebound. The near-term path hinges on 
five factors: 1) the 3 November US mid-term election, where 
a ‘Democratic sweep’ would likely be bond -supportive by 
constraining US President Trump’s fiscal spending agenda , 
while a ‘Republican sweep’ would likely weigh on bonds as 
markets price in further fiscal expansion and a higher term 
premium; 2) Fed communication at upcoming policy meetings; 
3) the US Treasury’s quarterly refunding announcement on 4 
November, where a ny signal of reduced long -end issuance  
would likely compress long-end yields; 4) softening auction 
demand and repatriation by foreign investors, which add 
uncertainty around demand for US government bonds; and 5) 
the f luid geopolitical situation in the Middle East , which is 
keeping oil prices elevated, with a risk of further upside. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect moderating oil prices and the 
fading inflationary impact of tariffs to give the Fed room to 
consider rate cuts. The US 30-year yield, however, is likely to 
stay elevated as fiscal deficit remains substantial next year.  
We expect a final ECB hike to 2.75% by December 2026, then 
a pause in H1 2027, followed by a 25bps cut in Q3 2027. In 
Japan, we expect quarterly pace of BoJ hikes, with one hike 
that takes the policy rate to 1.50% by December 2026 and two 
more in H1 2027, reaching 2.00% before pausing at mid-year.

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10 
DM corporates – Core holding 
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across DM credit. Credit spreads have widened from 
their lows but remain close to historical tights, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight-spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion,  
especially in the IG space, where heavy AI-related issuance 
is weighing on the long end of the curve. AI -driven supply is 
surpassing USD 2 50bn in 2026 and mak ing up a growing 
share of US IG gross issuance, but there are growing signs 
that hyperscalers are turning to private markets for fundin g, 
which should help ease public bond market supply pressures.  
We remain selective at the long end  of the yield curve and  
favour a duration of  3-7 years, where risk -adjusted carry 
remains most durable. Issuance-driven concessions in 
sectors with strong capex cycles (notably technology, capital 
goods and basic industries) present opportunities for investors 
who can stomach near-term volatility. 
Fig. 12 DM IG spreads have widened from their lows, 
but remain near historically tight levels 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
DM HY spreads  have widened to roughly a six-month high. 
Persistent doubts about whether hyperscalers can generate 
adequate returns on massive AI capex investments triggered 
a broad risk asset sell-off. The CCC tier has become the focal 
point of genuine credit concern, with yields hovering near the 
high-15% range and spreads near a four-year high. Market 
focus has shifted to the funding pressures facing AI-related 
neocloud companies. We view this  as idiosyncratic for now 
and see a limited impact on the broader HY universe.  The 
underlying credit picture is mixed. Leverage is elevated and 
interest coverage is thin, but the broad market has not yet 
shown signs of systemic stress. The US HY d efault rate  
remains below trend in 2026. 
HY offers an attractive income proposition, with all-in yields of 
over 8%. Its shorter-duration profile, combined with its lower 
sensitivity to changes in the term premium and rate volatility, 
make it an attractive sleeve in the current phase of the cycle.  
We remain attentive to evolving credit rating migration trends 
and sector-specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades 
slow and new issuance clusters around key themes. However, 
healthy issuer fu ndamentals and stable credit metrics  
continue to support it as a Core holding allocation. 
Fig. 13 DM HY spreads are off the tights 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight 
USD/Core holding for LCY 
We are Overweight EM USD government bonds and retain 
EM LCY government bonds as a Core holding, reflecting a 
balanced perspective across both regional performance and 
currency dynamics. In EM USD sovereign debt, the recent 
spread widening offered an attractive entry point to gain 
exposure to areas benefiting from resilient global growth and 
stable external conditions. Technicals remain favourable, with 
supply being front-loaded and investor positioning remaining 
relatively light compared with historical levels. 
Many EM central banks having tightened earlier and more 
aggressively post -pandemic, preserving credibility and 
maintaining positive real rates. External balances have 
improved, reserve buffers have increased and greater 
reliance on domestic funding reduces external vulnerability. 
Lula’s surprise win in Brazil’s presidential election raises 
political risk. The country’s fiscal trajectory under the incoming 
administration is a key risk to monitor. Fiscal slippage could  
trigger a broader repricing of LATAM risk. In Asia, higher-rated 
issuers continue to offer a valuable buffer during bouts of 
global rate volatility, while select frontier exposures in EMEA 
remain more sensitive to liquidity shifts and changes in risk 
appetite. Index composition also shapes resilience: the EM 
USD benchmark is less exposed to oil price shocks , as 
countries that are not oil importers account for a larger share 
of the benchmark than in  the LCY benchmark. We expect 
returns to be primarily driven by carry rather than further 
spread compression.
